\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{t}|(B^{n}(\overline{U}_{n})-B(\overline{U}),z)_{H}|dr

(4.79)

\displaystyle\leq\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}|(B^{n}(\overline{U}_{n})-B(\overline{U}_{n}),z)_{H}|dr}_{J_{n}^{3,1}}+\underbrace{\int_{0}^{t}|(B(\overline{U}_{n})-B(\overline{U}),z)_{H}|dr}_{J_{n}^{3,2}}.

Remark that

\displaystyle J_{n}^{3,1}\leq\int_{0}^{T}|(B(\overline{U}_{n}),P^{n}(z)-z)_{H}|dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|B(\overline{U}_{n})\|_{V^{\prime}}\|P^{n}(z)-z\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3})}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|\overline{U}_{n}\|_{V}^{2}\|P^{n}(z)-z\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3})}dr

\displaystyle\leq C\|P^{n}(z)-z\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3})},

(4.80)

using the energy estimate (
4.29
). Therefore
\mathbbm{E}[J_{n}^{3,1}]\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.
Moreover, since
B(\overline{U}_{n})-B(\overline{U})=B(\overline{U}_{n}-\overline{U},\overline{U}_{n})-B(\overline{U},\overline{U}_{n}-\overline{U})
, use Cauchy-Schwarz inequality to obtain

\displaystyle J_{n}^{3,2}

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{t}\|\overline{U}_{n}\|_{H}\|\overline{U}_{n}-\overline{U}\|_{H}\|z\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3})}dr+C\int_{0}^{t}\|\overline{U}\|_{H}\|\overline{U}_{n}-\overline{U}\|_{H}\|z\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3})}dr

(4.81)

\displaystyle\leq C[\|\overline{U}\|_{L^{2}([0,T],H)}+\sup_{n\geq 0}\|\overline{U}_{n}\|_{L^{2}([0,T],H)}]\|\overline{U}_{n}-\overline{U}\|_{L^{2}([0,T],H)}\|z\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3})},

and thus
\mathbbm{E}[J_{n}^{3,2}]\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0
. Gathering the estimates on
J_{n}^{3,1}
and
J_{n}^{3,2}
,
\overline{\mathbbm{P}}
-a.s yields,

J_{n}^{3}\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{t}(B(\overline{U}),z)_{H}dr.

Furthermore, we show that
J_{n}^{7}
and
J_{n}^{8}
converge in probability. First of all, we show the convergence of
J_{n}^{7}
in probability. By the triangle inequality,

\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U}_{n})-G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))}^{2}\leq 2\underbrace{\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U}_{n})-G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))}^{2}}_{\leq C\|\overline{U}-\overline{U}_{n}\|_{H}^{2}}\\
+2\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U})-G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))}^{2},

and we remark that

\displaystyle\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U})-G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))}^{2}=\|(P^{n}-I)G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{n+1}^{-3}\|G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{n+1}^{-3}(1+\|\overline{U}\|_{H}^{2}).

We deduce

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U})-G_{\sigma}\overline{U})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},\mathcal{D}(A^{-3})}^{2}dr\Big]\leq C\lambda_{n+1}^{-3}(1+\mathbbm{E}[\|\overline{U}\|_{H}^{2}])

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{n+1}^{-3}(1+\mathbbm{E}[\sup_{[0,T]}\|\overline{U}\|_{H}^{2}])

\displaystyle\leq C\lambda_{n+1}^{-3}\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}0.

Therefore we obtain the following convergence:

G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U}_{n})\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})
, in probability. By Lemma 2.1 of
[
17
]
, we reach

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}G_{\sigma}^{n}(\overline{U}_{n})d\overline{W}_{r}^{n}\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{t}G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})d\overline{W}_{r}\text{ in probability in $L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{L}_{2}(L^{1}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3}),\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))$}.

(4.82)

Using similar arguments, we also reach

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{t}\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{r}^{\sigma,{\overline{U}_{n}}}\Big)^{n}\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}\int_{0}^{t}\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{r}^{\sigma,{\overline{U}}}\text{ in probability in $L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{L}_{2}(L^{1}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3}),\mathcal{D}(A^{-3}))$}.

(4.83)

Thus,

\displaystyle J_{n}^{7}\overset{L^{1}}{\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}}\int_{0}^{t}(G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})d\overline{W}_{r},z)_{H},\text{ and }\hskip 9.24994ptJ_{n}^{8}\overset{L^{1}}{\underset{n\longrightarrow\infty}{\longrightarrow}}\int_{0}^{t}(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{r}^{\sigma,{\overline{U}}},z)_{H}.

(4.84)

Consequently, there exists a subsequence of the family
\overline{U}_{n}
such that
J_{n}^{7}
and
J_{n}^{8}
converge almost surely to
\int_{0}^{t}(G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})d\overline{W}_{r},z)_{H}
and
\int_{0}^{t}(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{r}^{\sigma,{\overline{U}}},z)_{H}
, respectively.

By taking the limit in the Galerkin equation for such a subsequence, for all
z\in\mathcal{D}(A^{3})
, the following holds,

\Big(\overline{U}-\overline{U}(0)+\int_{0}^{t}[A\overline{U}+B(\overline{U})+\Gamma\overline{U}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{\overline{U}}+F_{\sigma}(\overline{U})]dr,z\Big)_{H}\\
=\int_{0}^{t}\Big(G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})d\overline{W}_{r}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{r}^{\sigma,\overline{U}},z\Big)_{H}.

(4.85)

Hence, the following equality holds true almost surely in
\mathcal{D}(A^{-3})
,

\displaystyle d_{t}\overline{U}+[A\overline{U}+B(\overline{U})+\Gamma\overline{U}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{\overline{U}}+F_{\sigma}(\overline{U})]dt=G_{\sigma}(\overline{U})d\overline{W}_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,\overline{U}}.

(4.86)

Furthermore, as proven above,
\overline{U}_{n}
fulfils the energy estimates (
4.29
). Therefore, by taking the limit as
n\rightarrow 0
, we deduce

\displaystyle\overline{U}\in L^{2}(\overline{\Omega},L^{2}([0,T],V))\cap L^{2}(\overline{\Omega},L^{\infty}([0,T],H)).

(4.87)

\square

Consequently, Theorem
1
holds for
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
and
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
.

5
Existence and uniqueness of a local-in-time pathwise solution for the low-pass filtered problem

In this section, we show the second point of Theorem
1
, that is there exists a unique local-in-time pathwise solution to the (low-pass) filtered problem
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
– equation (
3.16
). The arguments can be adapted to the approximated problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
– equation (
3.22
), see Remark
5.1
in particular. In the following, using the method presented in
[
8
]
, we consider the following cut-off problem
(\mathcal{P}^{\kappa})
,

d_{t}X+[AX+\theta_{\kappa}(\|X-X^{ref}\|_{V})B(X)+\Gamma X+\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{X}\Big)+F_{\sigma}(X)]dt\\
=G_{\sigma}(X)dW_{t}-\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,X}\Big)\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathcal{P}^{\kappa}),

(5.1)

with the initial condition
U_{0}\in V
.

5.1
Energy estimate a cut-off system

In this subsection, we consider the projected cut-off problems
(\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\kappa})
,

d_{t}X_{n}+[AX_{n}+\theta_{\kappa}(\|X_{n}-X_{n}^{ref}\|_{V})B^{n}(X_{n})+\Gamma^{n}X_{n}+\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{X_{n}}\Big)^{n}+F_{\sigma}^{n}(X_{n})]dt\\
=G_{\sigma}^{n}(X_{n})dW_{t}-\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,X_{n}}\Big)^{n}\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\kappa}),

(5.2)

with the initial condition
U_{0}\in V
. The aim of this subsection is to prove that, for all
T>0
, there exists a constant
C_{\kappa,T}
, such that for all
n
,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|X_{n}\|_{V}^{2}]+\mathbbm{E}[\int_{0}^{T}\|X_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dt]\leq C_{\kappa,T}.

(5.3)

Define
X^{ref}
the unique
deterministic
solution to the heat equation,

d_{t}X^{ref}+AX^{ref}=0,\hskip 9.24994pt(X^{ref})_{t=0}=U_{0},

(5.4)

which belongs to
L^{\infty}\big([0,T],V\big)\cap L^{2}\big([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A)\big)
by a standard argument. We denote by
X_{n}^{ref}:=P^{n}X^{ref}
the projection of
X^{ref}
onto
H_{n}=P^{n}H
. Then, define the problems
(\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\kappa})
on
H_{n}
as follows, with the initial condition
(X_{n})_{t=0}=P^{n}U_{0}
,

d_{t}X_{n}+[AX_{n}+\theta_{\kappa}(\|X_{n}-X_{n}^{ref}\|_{V})B^{n}(X_{n})+\Gamma^{n}X_{n}+\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{X_{n}}\Big)^{n}+F_{\sigma}^{n}(X_{n})]dt\\
=G_{\sigma}^{n}(X_{n})dW_{t}-\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,X_{n}}\Big)^{n}\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\kappa}),

(5.5)

where
\theta_{\kappa}\in C^{\infty}(\mathbbm{R})
is such that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{1}_{[-\kappa/2,\kappa/2]}\leq\theta_{\kappa}\leq\mathbbm{1}_{[-\kappa,\kappa]},

(5.6)

and is referred to as the cut-off coefficient. Also,
p^{X_{n}}
and
p_{t}^{\sigma,X_{n}}
are respectively the pressure bounded variation and martingale terms associated to
X_{n}
. Now we infer that
\bar{X}_{n}=X_{n}-X_{n}^{ref}
fulfils

d_{t}\bar{X}_{n}+\Big[A\bar{X}_{n}+\theta_{\kappa}(\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V})B^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})+\Gamma^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})+\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\Big)^{n}\\
+F_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})\Big]dt=G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})dW_{t}-\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\Big)^{n},

(5.7)

with
(\bar{X}_{n})_{t=0}=0
. Remind that, for all
v^{\sharp}\in\mathcal{D}(A)
, the following holds

\displaystyle Av^{\sharp}=A^{v}v^{\sharp},\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{A}A^{v}v^{\sharp}=A^{v}\bar{v}^{\sharp},\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{R}A^{v}v^{\sharp}=A^{v}\tilde{v}^{\sharp}

(5.8)

Let
n
a positive integer, and
X_{n}
a solution of
(\mathcal{P}_{n}^{\kappa})
. Applying Itō’s lemma to equation (
5.7
) yields

\displaystyle d_{t}

\displaystyle\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-2\Big(A\bar{X}_{n},A\bar{X}_{n}+\theta_{\kappa}(\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V})B^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})+\Gamma^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})+\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\Big)^{n}\Big)_{H}dt

\displaystyle-2\Big(A\bar{X}_{n},F_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})\Big)_{H}dt+2\Big(A\bar{X}_{n},G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})dW_{t}-\Big(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\Big)^{n}\Big)_{H}

\displaystyle+\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}dt.

(5.9)

where
\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}
is defined similarly as in subsection
4.2
. Now we estimate each term in the previous relation. Begin with the advection term: by Young’s inequality, for all
\xi>0
there exists
C_{\xi}>0
such that

\theta_{\kappa}(\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V})(A\bar{X}_{n},B^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}))_{H}\\
\leq C\theta_{\kappa}(\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V})\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}+C\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}(\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{1/2}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{1/2}+1)\\
\leq C\kappa\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

(5.10)

where we used equation (
4.11
). In addition,

\displaystyle(\Gamma^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}),A\bar{X}_{n})_{H}\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{H}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

(5.11)

where the notation
C_{\xi}
is kept from line to line, as introduced in subsection
4.2
. For the bounded variation pressure term, we denote
\bar{X}_{n}=(v_{n}^{\bar{X}},T_{n}^{\bar{X}},S_{n}^{\bar{X}})^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}
, so that

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(A\bar{X}_{n},\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}

\displaystyle\nabla_{H}p^{\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\Big)_{H}\Big|\leq\Big|\Big(g\>\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}{(\beta_{T}T_{n}^{\bar{X}}+\beta_{S}S_{n}^{\bar{X}})dz^{\prime}},Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big(\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}K*\Big[v_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}w(v_{n}^{\bar{X}})+w_{s}\partial_{z}w(v_{n}^{\bar{X}})-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}w(v_{n}^{*,\bar{X}}))\Big],Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big(\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}K*\Big[v_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}w_{s}+w_{s}\partial_{z}w_{s}\Big]+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}w_{s})\Big]dz^{\prime},Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|+C_{n}^{ref}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|w(v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\|_{H}+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V})\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

(5.12)

where
C_{n}^{ref}
is a constant depending only on
\|X_{n}^{ref}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}
and the parameters of the problem. Notice that
C_{n}^{ref}
is bounded independently of
n
since
\|X_{n}^{ref}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}
is. To evaluate the noise associated drift term, we denote
D_{\mu,\nu}=Diag(\mu,\mu,\nu)
, so that, for
\xi>0
,

\displaystyle(F_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}),A\bar{X}_{n})_{H}

\displaystyle=\Big(d_{t}v_{s}+B(v_{n}^{\bar{X}},v_{s})-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}\nabla_{3}v_{s})+\big[Av_{s}+\Gamma v_{s}\big],Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}\Big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt+C_{n}^{ref2}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}\Big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|\bar{X}\|_{V})\|\bar{X}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(D_{\mu,\nu}a_{3}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(D_{\mu,\nu}a_{3}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}},

(5.13)

where we used the fact that
Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}=A^{v}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}
. Again,
C_{n}^{ref2}
is a constant that is bounded independently of
n
, and which depends only on
\|X_{n}^{ref}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}
and the parameters of the problem. To evaluate
\frac{1}{2}\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}
, we define

\displaystyle\psi_{k}=

\displaystyle-A\phi_{k}^{H}-B(\phi_{k},v_{s})-\Gamma\phi_{k}^{H}\in\mathcal{D}(A),

(5.14)

so that,

\frac{1}{2}\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}-\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}]-\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}^{\bar{X}}\|_{V}^{2}+C_{n}^{ref3}\\
\leq(\frac{1}{2}+\xi)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}-\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}]\|_{V}^{2}+C_{\xi}\Big(1+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}^{\bar{X}}\|_{V}^{2}\Big),

(5.15)

with
C_{n}^{ref3}
enjoying, again, the same properties as
C_{n}^{ref1}
and
C_{n}^{ref2}
. Consequently,

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}-\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}]\|_{V}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big(D_{\mu,\nu}a_{3}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}}+C(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2}),

(5.16)

and, using the regularisation property of
\mathcal{C}_{K}
– equation (
4.1
) – we deduce the following,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}^{\bar{X}}\|_{V}^{2}\leq C(1+\|w(v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\|_{H}^{2})

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|v_{n}^{\bar{X}}\|_{V}^{2}).

(5.17)

Therefore,

\frac{1}{2}\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}\leq\frac{1}{2}\Big(D_{\mu,\nu}a_{3}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}}\\
+C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}.

(5.18)

In addition, thanks to the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality, for all
T>0
,

\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\bigg|\int_{0}^{t}(A\bar{X}_{n},G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}})_{H}\bigg|\Bigg]\\
\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\underbrace{\|[G_{\sigma}^{n}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}]^{*}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}_{\leq C(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}\|_{V}^{2})}dr\Big)^{1/2}\Big]\\
\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\Big(\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dr\Big)^{1/2}\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\sqrt{1+\|\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}\|_{V}^{2}}\Big]\\
\leq\xi\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dr\Big]+C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2})\Big].

(5.19)

Gathering these estimates, we obtain on the RHS
2(C\kappa+4\xi)\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}
(see equation
5.10
). Thus, we choose
\xi=\frac{1}{16}
and
\kappa=\frac{1}{4C}
, so that, using analogous arguments as in section
4.2
, the following estimates hold for all
T>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\kappa,T},\hskip 9.24994pt\mathbbm{E}[\int_{0}^{T}\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dt]\leq C_{\kappa,T},

(5.20)

where
C_{\kappa,T}
is a constant independent of
n
.

Remark 5.1
.

Regarding the problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
, all the arguments are similar. In particular, the terms
(\hat{F}_{\sigma}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}),Av_{n}^{\bar{X}})_{H}
and
\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{G}_{\sigma}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}
can be estimated as follows,

\Big(\hat{F}_{\sigma}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}),Av_{n}^{\bar{X}}\Big)_{H}\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\Big(D_{\mu,\nu}a_{BT}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(D_{\mu,\nu}\overline{a_{BC}}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}\bar{v}_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}\bar{v}_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}},

(5.21)

and

\frac{1}{2}\|\hat{G}_{\sigma}(\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref})-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,\bar{X}_{n}+X_{n}^{ref}}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\bar{X}_{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\\
+\frac{1}{2}\Big(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}}+\frac{1}{2}\Big(\overline{a_{BC}}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}\bar{v}_{n}^{\bar{X}}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}\bar{v}_{n}^{\bar{X}})\Big)_{L^{2}}.

(5.22)

Therefore, we conclude that the existence of a pathwise solution of
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
holds in the same space, adapting the rest of the proof being immediate.

5.2
Uniqueness of the solution

In this subsection, we show that pathwise solutions to the problem
(\mathcal{P}^{\kappa})
are unique. Our proof is analogous to the one of Proposition 3.5 in
[
8
]
.

Let
(\mathcal{S},X^{1})
and
(\mathcal{S},X^{2})
two pathwise solutions of
(\mathcal{P}^{\kappa})
on the same stochastic basis. Let
R:=X^{1}-X^{2}
. Then remark that, for all
T>0
,

\displaystyle R\in L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))\cap L^{\infty}([0,T],V).

(5.23)

Substract the equations that
X^{1}
and
X^{2}
fulfil, so that
R
is a solution of the problem

d_{t}R+ARdt+\Big[B(X^{1},X^{1})-B(X^{2},X^{2})\Big]dt+C(R)dt+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(p^{X^{1}}-p^{X^{2}})dt\\
+[F_{\sigma}(R)-F_{\sigma}(0)]dt=[G_{\sigma}(R)-G_{\sigma}(0)]dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(dp_{t}^{\sigma,X^{1}}-dp_{t}^{\sigma,X^{2}}),

(5.24)

with
R(0)=X^{1}(0)-X^{2}(0).
Using Itō’s lemma, we reach

\displaystyle d_{t}\|R\|_{H}^{2}=

\displaystyle-2(R,AR+B(X^{1})-B(X^{2})+C(R)+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(p^{X^{1}}-p^{X^{2}}))_{H}dt

(5.25)

\displaystyle+2(R,[F_{\sigma}(R)-F_{\sigma}(0)])_{H}dt+\|[G_{\sigma}(R)-G_{\sigma}(0)]-[\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,X^{1}}-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,X^{2}}]\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H)}^{2}dt

\displaystyle+2(R,[G_{\sigma}(R)-G_{\sigma}(0)]dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(dp_{t}^{\sigma,X^{1}}-dp_{t}^{\sigma,X^{2}}))_{H}.

Let
\eta,\zeta
two stopping times such that with
0\leq\eta<\zeta<\tau
. Integrate between
\eta
and
\zeta
, then take the supremum and the expectation, so that

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}

\displaystyle\|R\|_{H}^{2}+c\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{V}^{2}ds\Big]\leq\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|R(\eta)\|_{H}^{2}\Big]+2\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}|(R,B(X^{1})-B(X^{2}))_{H}|ds\Big]

\displaystyle+2\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}|(R,CR+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(p^{X^{1}}-p^{X^{2}})+[F_{\sigma}(R)-F_{\sigma}(0)])_{H}|ds\Big]

\displaystyle+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|[G_{\sigma}(R)-G_{\sigma}(0)]-[\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,X^{1}}-\mathcal{M}^{\sigma,X^{2}}]\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H)}^{2}ds\Big]

\displaystyle+2\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{t}(R,[G_{\sigma}(R)-G_{\sigma}(0)]dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(dp_{t}^{\sigma,X^{1}}-dp_{t}^{\sigma,X^{2}}))_{H}\Big|\Big]

\displaystyle:=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}+J_{4}.

(5.26)

For
J_{1}
, by Hölder’s inequality,

\displaystyle J_{1}

\displaystyle=2\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}|(R,B(R,X^{1}))_{H}+(R,B(X^{2},R))_{H}|ds\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{H}\|R\|_{V}\Big(\|X^{1}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}+\|X^{2}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}\Big)ds\Big]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{H}^{2}\Big(\|X^{1}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+\|X^{2}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\Big)ds\Big]+\xi\mathbbm{E}[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{V}^{2}ds].

(5.27)

For
J_{2}
and
J_{3}
, repeating the arguments of subsection
5.1
,

\displaystyle J_{2}+J_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{H}^{2}\Big]+\xi\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{V}^{2}\Big].

(5.28)

For
J_{4}
,

J_{4}\leq C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\Big(R,\big[G_{\sigma}R-G_{\sigma}(0)\big]\phi_{k}-\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}[\pi_{k}^{U_{1}}-\pi_{k}^{U_{2}}]\Big)_{H}^{2}\>dr\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]\\
\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{H}^{2}\Big]+\xi\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{V}^{2}\Big].

(5.29)

Then, using the stochastic Grönwall lemma (see Lemma 5.3 in
[
28
]
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,T]}\|R\|_{H}^{2}\Big]=0.

(5.30)

Therefore,
R=0
a.s., so the solution to
(\mathcal{P}^{\kappa})
is pathwise unique.

\square

5.3
Maximal solution with improved regularity

Using similar tightness arguments as in subsection
4.3
, together with a Gyöngy-Krylov argument as in
[
8
,
17
]
(see also
[
31
]
), we deduce that there exists an unique solution
X
to
(\mathcal{P}^{\kappa})
, such that
X\in L^{\infty}([0,T],V)\cap L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))
almost surely. In addition, the convergence
X_{n}\rightarrow X
happens in probability in
L^{2}([0,T],V)
. Hence, by setting

\tau^{\kappa}:=\Big\{t>0\Big|\|X-X_{ref}\|_{V}\geq\kappa/2\Big\},

(5.31)

we infer that
U=X_{\tau^{\kappa}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot}}
is a (pathwise unique) local-in-time solution to the problem
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
. By repeating the arguments of
[
28
]
, we conclude that there exists a maximal solution
U
to
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
, associated to the stopping time
\tau
. Moreover, this solution is global-in-time whenever the following holds,

\forall T>0,\hskip 9.24994pt\sup_{[0,\tau\wedge T]}\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\|U\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}<\infty,\text{ a.s.}

(5.32)

In the rest of the subsection, we show the following result on time-continuity
U
, that is, for all stopping time
0<\tau^{\prime}<\tau
and
T>0
,

U_{\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot}}^{*}\in C([0,T],V).

(5.33)

For this purpose, we use an argument proposed in
[
17
,
19
]
. Consider the following equation,

d_{t}z(t)+Az(t)dt+\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p^{U^{*}}dt=G_{\sigma}(U^{*})d\overline{W}_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma,U^{*}},

(5.34)

with the initial condition
z(0)=U^{*}(0)
, and keeping the same notation
U^{*}
as before. Again,
p^{U^{*}}
and
p_{t}^{\sigma,U^{*}}
are respectively the pressure bounded variation and martingale terms associated to
U^{*}
. This equation has a unique solution satisfying
z(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})\in L^{2}(\Omega,C([0,T],V))
.

In addition, using Itō’s lemma with
x\mapsto\frac{1}{2}\|x\|_{V}^{2}
, the derivation of the following estimate is straightforward,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\sup_{0\leq t\leq T}\|z(\tau^{\prime}\wedge t)\|_{V}^{2}]+\mathbbm{E}[\int_{0}^{T}\|z(\tau^{\prime}\wedge t)\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dt]<\infty.

(5.35)

Thus,
z\in L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))
almost surely. Let
\tilde{U}:=U^{*}-z\in L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))
, so that

d_{t}\tilde{U}+A\tilde{U}dt+B(\tilde{U}+z)dt+C(\tilde{U}+z)dt+F_{\sigma}(\tilde{U}+z)dt=0.

(5.36)

Since
\tilde{U},z\in L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))
, we have

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|B(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{H}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{V}^{2}\|(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}

(5.37)

\displaystyle\leq C\Big(\sup_{[0,T]}\|(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{V}^{2}\Big)\int_{0}^{T}\|(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\leq C,

and

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|A\tilde{U}(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{H}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|\tilde{U}(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\leq C,

(5.38)

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{T}\|F_{\sigma}(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{H}^{2}ds

\displaystyle\leq C\int_{0}^{T}\|(\tilde{U}+z)(\tau^{\prime}\wedge s)\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\leq C.

(5.39)

Therefore
A\tilde{U}_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})},B(\tilde{U}+z)_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})},F_{\sigma}(\tilde{U}+z)_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})}\in L^{2}([0,T],H)
almost surely, consequently
(\frac{d}{dt}\tilde{U})_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})}\in L^{2}([0,T],H)
almost surely as well. Since
\tilde{U}\in L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))

\overline{\mathbbm{P}}
-a.s, we can apply a result from
[
54
]
(Chapter 3, Lemma 1.2), to reach

\displaystyle\tilde{U}_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})}\in L^{2}(\Omega,C([0,T],V)).

(5.40)

As a consequence

\displaystyle z_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})},\tilde{U}_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})},U^{*}_{(\tau^{\prime}\wedge\boldsymbol{\cdot})}\in L^{2}(\Omega,L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A)))\cap L^{2}(\Omega,C([0,T],V)).

(5.41)

\square

Consequently, Theorem
1
holds for
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
. As shown in the previous remarks, the same holds true for
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
, with minor changes in the proof.

6
Existence of a global-in-time pathwise solution under further assumptions

In this section, we show that the approximated low-pass filtered problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
– equation (
3.22
) – admits a global pathwise solution, assuming
K\in H^{15/4}
. This implies that
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
is well-posed as well for a purely barotropic noise
\sigma dW_{t}
, since it corresponds to
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
with
\sigma_{BC}dW_{t}=0
. As we assume
K\in H^{15/4}
, the functional
\mathcal{C}_{K}[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]=K*[\boldsymbol{\cdot}]
fulfils

\|\mathcal{C}_{K}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\|_{H^{k}}\leq C(1+\|\boldsymbol{\cdot}\|_{L^{2}}),\hskip 9.24994pt\forall k\in\{0,1,2,3\}.

(6.1)

This remark will be used further to establish energy estimates. In addition, we remind that the noise is assumed to be regular enough in the sense of equation (
3.1
), that is

\displaystyle\sup_{t\in[0,T]}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3})}^{2}<\infty,

(6.2)

\displaystyle u_{s}\in L^{\infty}\Big([0,T],H^{4}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3})\Big),

\displaystyle d_{t}u_{s}\in L^{\infty}\Big([0,T],H^{3}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3})\Big),

\displaystyle a\nabla u_{s}\in L^{\infty}\Big([0,T],H^{2}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3})\Big).

Let
U
a maximal pathwise solution of
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
, and denote by
\tau
its associated stopping time. As mentioned in subsection
5.3
, it suffices to show that equation (
5.32
) holds true. The following proof relies on a decomposition of the velocity between the barotropic and the baroclinic modes, which originates from
[
10
]
. Remind first that the barotropic and baroclinic operators
\mathcal{A}_{2}
and
\mathcal{R}
are defined as

\displaystyle(\mathcal{A}_{2}v)(x,y)=\frac{1}{h}\int_{-h}^{0}v(x,y,z^{\prime})\>dz^{\prime},\hskip 9.24994pt(\mathcal{A}v)(x,y,z)=(\mathcal{A}_{2}v)(x,y),\hskip 9.24994pt\mathcal{R}v=(I-\mathcal{A})v.

(6.3)

Then, decompose the velocity
v
as follows

\displaystyle v=\bar{v}+\tilde{v},\hskip 9.24994pt\bar{v}=\mathcal{A}_{2}v,\hskip 9.24994pt\tilde{v}=\mathcal{R}v.

(6.4)

Using the same computation as in
[
10
]
, one shows that the following holds on the (2D) domain
\mathcal{S}_{H}
for
\bar{v}
, using that
\overline{\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p}=\mathbf{P}_{2D}\nabla_{H}\bar{p}=0
, and similarly that
\overline{\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma}}=0
,

d_{t}\bar{v}+\mathbf{P}_{2D}\Big[-\mu_{v}\Delta_{H}\bar{v}+(\bar{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\bar{v}+\mathcal{A}_{2}[(\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\tilde{v}+(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\tilde{v})\tilde{v}]+f\vec{k}\times\bar{v}\Big]dt\\
+\mathcal{A}_{2}\hat{F}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)dt=\mathcal{A}_{2}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)dW_{t},

(6.5)

with
\bar{v}_{0}=\mathcal{A}_{2}v_{0}
,
\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\bar{v}=0
on
\mathcal{S}_{H}
and
\bar{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\vec{n}=\frac{\partial\bar{v}}{\vec{n}}\times\vec{n}=0
on
\partial\mathcal{S}_{H}
. Moreover,
\tilde{v}
fulfils the following on
\mathcal{S}

d_{t}\tilde{v}+\Big[A\tilde{v}+B(\tilde{v})+(\bar{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\tilde{v}+(\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\bar{v}-\mathcal{A}[(\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\tilde{v}+(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\tilde{v})\tilde{v}]+f\vec{k}\times\tilde{v}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p}\Big]dt\\
+\mathcal{R}\hat{F}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)dt=\mathcal{R}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\widetilde{\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma}},

(6.6)

with
\tilde{v}_{0}=\mathcal{R}v_{0}
, and
\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\vec{n}=\frac{\partial\bar{v}}{\vec{n}}\times\vec{n}=0
on
\Gamma_{l}
,
\partial_{z}\tilde{v}=0
on
\Gamma_{h}\cup\Gamma_{b}
. In addition, by the energy estimate (
4.29
) of section
4
, the following estimate holds for all
p\geq 2
and all
T>0
,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{s\in[0,T\wedge\tau]}\|U_{s}\|_{H}^{p}+\int_{0}^{T\wedge\tau}\|U_{s}\|_{H}^{p-2}\|U_{s}\|_{V}^{2}dr\Bigg]\leq C.

(6.7)

6.1
H^{1}
estimate for the barotropic velocity

Apply Itō’s lemma with the function
\|\mathcal{A}A^{1/2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\|_{H}^{2}
to the problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
, so that

\displaystyle d_{t}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}dt\leq

\displaystyle-2\Big(\mathbf{P}_{2D}\mathcal{A}_{2}[(\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\tilde{v}+\tilde{v}(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\tilde{v})],A\bar{v}\Big)_{H}dt

(6.8)

\displaystyle-2\Big(\mathbf{P}_{2D}(\bar{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\bar{v},A\bar{v}\Big)_{H}dt-2\Big(f\vec{k}\times\bar{v},A\bar{v}\Big)_{H}dt-2\Big(\mathcal{A}_{2}\hat{F}_{\sigma}^{v}(U),A\bar{v}\Big)_{H}dt

\displaystyle+\Big\|\mathcal{A}_{2}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}dt+2\sum_{k=1}^{\infty}\Big(\mathcal{A}_{2}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\phi_{k},A\bar{v}\Big)_{H}d\beta^{k}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{5}I_{j}\>dt+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta^{k}.

Using the same arguments as in
[
10
]
, we have

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq C\Big(\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}\Big)^{1/2}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ and }\hskip 9.24994ptI_{2}\leq C\|\bar{v}\|_{H}^{1/2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{3/2}.

(6.9)

Hence, by Young’s inequality, for all
\xi>0
, there exists
C_{\xi}>0
such that

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq\xi\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}+C_{\xi}\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S},\hskip 9.24994pt\text{ and }\hskip 9.24994ptI_{2}

\displaystyle\leq\xi\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}+C_{\xi}\|U\|_{H}^{2}\|U\|_{V}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2},

(6.10)

using that
\|\bar{v}\|_{V}\leq\|U\|_{V}
. Once again, the notation
C_{\xi}
is carried from line to line, even if it may refer to different
\xi
-dependent constants. Furthermore,

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq\xi\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}+C_{\xi}\|U\|_{H}^{2},

(6.11)

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\xi\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}+C_{\xi}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})-\mu(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}),A\bar{v})_{L^{2}}-(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\overline{a_{BC}}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}),A\bar{v})_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\xi\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}+C_{\xi}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})-\mu(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}),A\bar{v})_{L^{2}}-\underbrace{(\overline{a_{BC}}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}\bar{v}),\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}\bar{v}))_{L^{2}}}_{\geq 0}.

(6.12)

For
I_{5}
, define
\psi_{k}=-\mathcal{A}_{2}(A\phi_{k}^{H}+B(\phi_{k},v_{s})+\Gamma\phi_{k}^{H})
, so we have

\displaystyle\|\mathcal{A}_{2}

\displaystyle G_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}=\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}-\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}^{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}\bar{v}]-\mathbf{P}_{2D}\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)\|_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}\|_{V}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}^{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}\bar{v}+\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)]\|_{V}^{2}+2\underbrace{|(\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}^{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}\bar{v}+\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)],\psi_{k})_{V}|}_{\leq|(b(\phi_{k},v,\Delta_{3}\psi_{k})|\leq\|\phi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\psi_{k}\|_{H^{2}}\|U\|_{V}}

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}+\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)]\|_{V}^{2}+C(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2}).

(6.13)

Moreover, remark that

\displaystyle\|\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}^{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}\bar{v}+\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)]\|_{V}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\|\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}]\|_{V}^{2}+\xi\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}

\displaystyle+C_{\xi}\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}^{2}(\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}+\|v\|_{V}^{2}+1).

In addition, since

\|\mathbf{P}_{2D}[\bar{\phi}_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}]\|_{V}^{2}\leq\mu(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\bar{\phi}_{k}\bar{\phi}_{k}^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}),A\bar{v})_{L^{2}}+C_{\xi}\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}^{2}(\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

we derive

\displaystyle I_{5}

\displaystyle\leq\mu(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}),A\bar{v})_{L^{2}}+C_{\xi}(\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+1)+\xi\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}.

(6.14)

Gathering the previous estimates into the Itō lemma equation (
6.8
), then time integrating between two stopping times
0<\eta<\zeta<T\wedge\tau
, and taking the expectation, we reach

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}(\zeta)\Big]+(c-\alpha_{1})\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\bar{v}(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}+1\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|U\|_{H}^{2}\|U\|_{V}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}ds\Big],

(6.15)

for some
\alpha_{1}\in(0,c)
. Similarly, time integrating, then taking the supremum over
[\eta,\zeta]
and the expectation, we have also

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+(c-\alpha_{1})\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\bar{v}(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}+1\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|U\|_{H}^{2}\|U\|_{V}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}ds\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg].

(6.16)

Additionally, by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (Theorem 4.36 in
[
16
]
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}

\displaystyle\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big|\Big(\psi_{k}-\bar{\phi}_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla\bar{v}-\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v),A\bar{v}\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}|(\psi_{k},\bar{v})_{V}|^{2}+|(\bar{\phi}_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla\bar{v},(-\Delta_{H})\bar{v})_{L^{2}}|^{2}+|(\mathcal{A}_{2}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v),A\bar{v})_{L^{2}}|^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}\|_{V}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|\bar{\phi}_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|\phi_{k}^{z}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq\xi\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[1+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}ds\Bigg].

(6.17)

Therefore, by (
6.15
), we reach

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|A\bar{v}\|_{H}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C_{1}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\bar{v}(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}+1\Big]\\
+C_{1}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|U\|_{H}^{2}\|U\|_{V}^{2}\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{V}^{2}ds\Big].

(6.18)

6.2
L^{2}
estimate for the vertical gradient of velocity

Now remark that

\displaystyle\partial_{z}[(v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})v+w(v)\partial_{z}v]

\displaystyle=(\partial_{z}v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})v+(v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\partial_{z}v-(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}v)\partial_{z}v+w(v)\partial_{zz}v,

(6.19)

and

\displaystyle((v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\partial_{z}v+w(v)\partial_{zz}v,\partial_{z}v)_{L^{2}}=0.

So

\displaystyle(\partial_{z}[(v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})v+w(v)\partial_{z}v],\partial_{z}v)_{H}=((\partial_{z}v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})v-(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}v)\partial_{z}v,\partial_{z}v)_{H}.

(6.20)

Applying Itō’s lemma with
\|\partial_{z}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\|_{H}^{2}
to the problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
, we reach

\displaystyle d_{t}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+2

\displaystyle c\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\leq-2\Big((\partial_{z}v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})v-(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}v)\partial_{z}v+g[\beta_{T}\nabla_{H}T+\beta_{S}\nabla_{H}S],\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle+\Big(\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\Big[\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s}))+K*[(u_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3})(w(v)+w_{s})]\Big],\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle-2\Big(\partial_{z}\hat{F}_{\sigma}^{v}(U),\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}dt+\Big\|\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)-\partial_{z}\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}\Big\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},{L^{2}})}^{2}dt

\displaystyle+2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}\Big[\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)(\phi_{k})-\partial_{z}{\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}}\Big]\boldsymbol{\cdot}\partial_{z}v\>d\beta_{t}^{k}

\displaystyle:=\sum_{j=1}^{4}I_{j}dt+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{5}^{k}\>d\beta_{t}^{k},

(6.21)

with
c=\min_{i}\{\mu_{i},\nu_{i}\}
for example, since
(f\vec{k}\times\partial_{z}v,\partial_{z}v)_{H}=0
. Repeating the argument of
[
33
]
(Step 2, p.24), for all
\xi>0
,

\displaystyle I_{1}\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|v\|_{V}^{2})\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C_{\xi}\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\xi\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}.

(6.22)

For
I_{2}
, by the regularising properties of
K
and Sobolev embeddings, since
u_{s}
and
\phi_{k}
are divergence-free,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle=\Big(\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\Big[\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s}))+K*[u_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s})]\Big],\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}

(6.23)

\displaystyle\leq\sum_{k}\|\nabla_{H}[\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\phi_{k}\mathcal{C}_{K}^{*}\mathcal{C}_{K}[\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(w(v)+w_{s})\phi_{k}])\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}+\|K\|_{H^{2}}\|u_{s}\|_{H^{3/2}}\|w(v)\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq\Big(\|K\|_{H^{2}}^{2}(\|w_{s}\|_{L^{2}}+\|w(v)\|_{L^{2}})\sum_{k}\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}^{2}+\|K\|_{H^{2}}\|u_{s}\|_{H^{3/2}}\|w(v)\|_{L^{2}}\Big)\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}.

\displaystyle\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\hskip 18.49988pt\leq C(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2}).

In addition,

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle\leq C-(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v,\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v)_{L^{2}}

(6.24)

\displaystyle I_{4}

\displaystyle\leq\Big[C+\underbrace{\Big(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\partial_{z}\phi_{k}^{BC}(\partial_{z}\phi_{k}^{BC})^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}\nabla_{3}v,\nabla_{3}v\Big)_{L^{2}}}_{\leq C\|U\|_{V}^{2}}+(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v,\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v)_{L^{2}}\Big]

(6.25)

\displaystyle+\underbrace{\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\nabla_{H}\Big[K*[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s})]+A\phi_{k}^{z}\Big]\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}_{\leq C(1+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}K)*(\phi_{k}w(v))\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\leq C(1+\|K\|_{W^{2,\infty}}^{2}\|v\|_{V}^{2})}

\displaystyle+\Big|\underbrace{2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\partial_{z}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v+\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{s}+A\phi_{k}^{H}+\Gamma\phi_{k}^{H}],\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}[K*[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s})]+A\phi_{k}^{z}])_{L^{2}}}_{\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|v\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}\Big|.

Gathering the previous estimates, integrating and taking expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(\zeta)\Big]+(c-\alpha_{2})\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}

\displaystyle\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\partial_{z}v(\eta)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\Big]

(6.26)

\displaystyle+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}ds\Big].

Similarly, by taking the supremum, we reach

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big]+(c-\alpha_{2})\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\partial_{z}v(\eta)\|_{H}^{2}+1\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}ds\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg].

(6.27)

Denoting
\psi_{k}=\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{s}]+A\phi_{k}^{H}+C\phi_{k}^{H}
and
\psi_{k}^{w}=\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w_{s}+A\phi_{k}^{z}
, and using the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (Theorem 4.36 in
[
16
]
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[

\displaystyle\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big|\Big(\partial_{z}\psi_{k}-\partial_{z}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v)-\partial_{z}{\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}},\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big|\Big(\partial_{z}\psi_{k}+\partial_{z}\psi_{k}^{w}-\partial_{z}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v)+\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}K*(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w(v)),\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg].

(6.28)

Remark that

\Big|\Big(\partial_{z}\psi_{k}+\partial_{z}\psi_{k}^{w}-\partial_{z}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v)+\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}K*(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w(v)),\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}\\
\leq C\Big[\|\partial_{z}\psi_{k}+\partial_{z}\psi_{k}^{w}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\phi_{k}\|_{W^{1,\infty}}^{2}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\Big|\Big(\nabla_{H}K*[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w(v)],\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}\Big],

and that

\displaystyle\Big(\nabla_{H}K*[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w(v)],\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\leq C\|w(v)\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\|v\|_{V}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})\leq C(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}).

Consequently,

C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big|\Big(\partial_{z}\psi_{k}+\partial_{z}\psi_{k}^{w}-\partial_{z}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v)+\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}K*(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w(v)),\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]\\
\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(1+\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}\Bigg)\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]\\
\leq\xi\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[1+\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Bigg]+C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})ds\Bigg].

(6.29)

Thus, by equation (
6.26
),

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C_{2}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|\partial_{z}v(\eta)\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1\Big]

(6.30)

\displaystyle+

\displaystyle C_{2}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}ds\Big].

6.3
L^{4}
estimate for the baroclinic velocity

Remind first that the Leray type projector
\mathbf{P}
corresponds to the identity on baroclinic vector fields. Also, using the regularising properties of the kernel
K
, we have

\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq C(1+\|w(v)\|_{H}^{2})\leq C(1+\|v\|_{V}^{2}).

(6.31)

Apply Itō’s lemma to the problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
with
\|\mathcal{R}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}
, so that

\displaystyle d_{t}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}

\displaystyle+4\int_{\mathcal{S}}(\mu_{v}|\nabla_{H}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\mu_{v}|\nabla_{H}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2}+\nu_{v}|\partial_{z}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\nu_{v}|\partial_{z}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2})d\mathcal{S}\>dt

(6.32)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\Big((\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\bar{v}-\mathcal{A}((\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})\tilde{v}+\tilde{v}(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\tilde{v}))+g\mathcal{R}\int_{z}^{0}(\beta_{T}\nabla_{H}T+\beta_{S}\nabla_{H}S)\>dz^{\prime}\Big)d\mathcal{S}\>dt

\displaystyle+4\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\mathcal{R}\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s}))+K*[u_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s})]\Big]dz^{\prime}

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\mathcal{R}\hat{F}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)d\mathcal{S}\>dt+2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\mathcal{R}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\phi_{k}-\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}\>dt

\displaystyle+4\int_{\mathcal{S}}|[\mathcal{R}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)-\mathcal{R}\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}]^{*}\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}\>dt+4\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\mathcal{R}G_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\phi_{k}-\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k})d\mathcal{S}\>d\beta^{k}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{5}I_{j}dt+\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta^{k},

remarking that
\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(f\vec{k}\times\tilde{v})=\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\bar{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}\tilde{v})=0
, since
\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\bar{v}=0
. For
I_{1}
, we reason similarly as in
[
10
]
(Section 3.2), with
L^{4}
rather than
L^{6}
, so that

\displaystyle|I_{1}|\leq\xi\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}+C_{\xi}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}(\|v\|_{H}^{2}\|v\|_{V}^{2}+1)+C\|T\|_{H}^{2}\|T\|_{V}^{2}.

(6.33)

For
I_{2}
, by Sobolev embedding,

\displaystyle I_{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\||\tilde{v}|^{2}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{1}}\|\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s}))+K*[u_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s})]\|_{L^{\infty}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|v\|_{V}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\Big(\|\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s}))\|_{H^{3/2}}+\|K*[u_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s})]\|_{H^{3/2}}\Big)

\displaystyle\leq C\|v\|_{V}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}(1+\|v\|_{H}\|K\|_{H^{3}}^{2}+\|v\|_{H}\|K\|_{H^{5/2}}\|u_{s}\|_{W^{1,\infty}})

\displaystyle\leq C\|v\|_{V}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}(1+\|U\|_{H})\leq C\|U\|_{V}^{2}(\|U\|_{H}^{2}+1)+C\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}.

(6.34)

For
I_{3}
,

\displaystyle I_{3}

\displaystyle=-4\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\Big[\mathcal{R}B(v,v_{s})-\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v})\Big]d\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle-4\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\mathcal{R}[d_{t}v_{s}+\frac{1}{2}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}v_{s})+Av_{s}+\Gamma v_{s}]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1)+2\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v})\>d\mathcal{S}+\xi\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}\>d\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1)-2\int_{\mathcal{S}}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}:\nabla_{3}\tilde{v})|\tilde{v}|^{2}-\underbrace{\frac{1}{2}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}|\tilde{v}|^{2})d\mathcal{S}}_{\geq 0}+\xi\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}\>d\mathcal{S}.

(6.35)

For
I_{4}
, define
\psi_{k}=\mathcal{R}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v+\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{s}+A\phi_{k}^{H}+\Gamma\phi_{k}^{H}]
and
\psi_{k}^{w}=\mathcal{R}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w_{s}+A\phi_{k}^{z}]
, so that

\displaystyle|I_{4}|

\displaystyle\leq 2\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}-\phi_{k}^{BT}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}-\phi_{k}^{BC}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}+\mathcal{A}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)-\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathcal{S}}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}:\nabla_{3}\tilde{v})\>|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}+2\int_{\mathcal{S}}|a_{BC}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}|\>|\nabla_{3}\bar{v}|\>|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}+\xi\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}\>|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle+\underbrace{C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\mathcal{A}\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{4}}^{2}}_{\leq C\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}}+C_{\xi}(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}\tilde{v}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1)+C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\underbrace{\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}}_{\leq C\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}}

\displaystyle\leq 2\int_{\mathcal{S}}(a_{BT}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}:\nabla_{3}\tilde{v})\>|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}+C_{\xi}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1)

\displaystyle+\xi\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|^{2}\>|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}+\xi\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

(6.36)

remarking that, by Hölder’s inequality, the 2D Sobolev embedding, and interpolation inequality,

\displaystyle 2\int_{\mathcal{S}}|a_{BC}\nabla_{3}\bar{v}|\>|\nabla_{3}\bar{v}|\>|\tilde{v}|^{2}d\mathcal{S}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\nabla_{3}\bar{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq C\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A^{3/4})}^{2}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\bar{v}\|_{V}\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\leq C\|U\|_{V}^{2}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\xi\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}.

(6.37)

For
I_{5}
, we have for any
\xi_{1}>0
,

\displaystyle|I_{5}|

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}(\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}-\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}+\mathcal{A}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)-\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}))^{2}

(6.38)

\displaystyle\leq 4\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}\underbrace{[\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}-\mathcal{A}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v))]^{2}}_{\leq\frac{1}{4}|\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2}+|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\mathcal{A}(\phi_{k}^{z}\partial_{z}v)|^{2}}d\mathcal{S}+C(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\|\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}+1)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{\mathcal{S}}|a_{3}\nabla_{3}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\>|\nabla_{3}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|d\mathcal{S}+C_{\xi_{1}}(\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+1)\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\xi_{1}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+C\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}.

Then, integrate and take the expectation, to reach

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\Big]+

\displaystyle(c-\alpha_{3})\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{S}}(|\nabla_{H}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+|\nabla_{H}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2})d\mathcal{S}\>ds\Big]

(6.39)

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi_{1}}

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|\tilde{v}(\eta)\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(\|U\|_{V}^{2}+1)\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\Big]+\xi_{1}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]

Similarly by taking the supremum,

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\Big]+(c-\alpha_{3})\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{S}}(|\nabla_{H}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+|\nabla_{H}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2})d\mathcal{S}\>ds\Big]\\
\leq C_{\xi_{1}}\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|\tilde{v}(\eta)\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(\|U\|_{V}^{2}+1)\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\Big]+\xi_{1}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\\
+\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg].

(6.40)

Also, by the Burkholder-Davis-Gundy inequality (Theorem 4.36 in
[
16
]
), one obtains

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}

\displaystyle\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{6}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg]\leq\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\mathcal{R}G_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\phi_{k}-\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}\Big)d\mathcal{S}\Big|^{2}ds\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\underbrace{\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\mathcal{R}G_{\sigma}^{v}(U)\phi_{k}\Big)d\mathcal{S}\Big|^{2}}_{\leq\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{2}\||\tilde{v}||\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|\|_{L^{2}}\leq C_{\xi}\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\xi\||\tilde{v}||\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|\|_{L^{2}}^{2}}+\underbrace{\Big|\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}\tilde{v}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big(\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}\Big)d\mathcal{S}\Big|^{2}}_{\leq\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{3}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\nabla_{H}\tilde{\pi}_{k}\|_{L^{4}}}ds\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+1)\Bigg]+\xi\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\||\tilde{v}||\nabla_{3}\tilde{v}|\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Bigg].

(6.41)

Therefore, using (
6.40
), the following result holds upon choosing
\xi_{1}=\frac{1}{16(C_{2}\vee 1)}
in equation (
6.38
),

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\int_{\mathcal{S}}(|\nabla_{H}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+|\nabla_{H}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+|\partial_{z}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|^{2})d\mathcal{S}\>ds\Big]\\
\leq C_{3}\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|\tilde{v}(\eta)\|_{L^{4}}^{4}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(\|U\|_{V}^{2}+1)\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4}\Big]+\frac{1}{16(C_{2}\vee 1)}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big].

(6.42)

6.4
Intermediate estimates

In this subsection, we gather the previous estimates on the vertical gradient and the barotropic and baroclinic modes of velocity, to obtain two intermediate estimates. This is used further to show the existence of a global pathwise solution. Define first, for
T>0
, and two stopping times
\eta<\zeta<T\wedge\tau
,

\displaystyle X_{s}:=\|\bar{v}\|_{V}^{2}+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|\tilde{v}\|_{L^{4}}^{4},\hskip 9.24994ptY_{s}:=\|\bar{v}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\tilde{v}|^{2}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2},

(6.43)

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\eta,\zeta):=\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{s\in[\eta,\zeta]}X_{s}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}Y_{s}ds\Big].

(6.44)

Then, gathering the previous estimates as follows:
\frac{\eqref{estimation-barotrope}}{8C_{1}C_{2}}+\frac{\eqref{estimation-gradient-z}}{4C_{2}}+\eqref{estimation-barocline}
, we reach

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(\eta,\zeta)\leq C\mathbbm{E}[1+X_{\eta}]+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U\|_{H}^{2})(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2})X_{s}\>ds\Big].

(6.45)

Define, for all
k>1
,

l_{k}:=\inf\Big\{t>0\>\Big|\>\|U(t)\|_{H}+(\int_{0}^{t}\|U\|_{V}^{2})^{1/2}\geq k\Big\}\wedge\tau,

(6.46)

so that
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathbbm{P}(l_{k}=\tau)=1
. Hence, using the stochastic Grönwall lemma of
[
28
]
with
\eta<\zeta<l_{k}\wedge T
, for all
k
there exists a
k
-dependent constant
C_{k}
, such that

\displaystyle\mathcal{E}(0,l_{k}\wedge T)\leq C_{k}(1+\|v_{0}\|_{V}^{4}).

(6.47)

Furthermore, by Itō’s lemma,

\displaystyle d_{t}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4}

\displaystyle+4c\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dt\leq-4\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}((\partial_{z}v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H})v-(\nabla_{H}\boldsymbol{\cdot}v)\partial_{z}v,\partial_{z}v)_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle+4\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(g[\beta_{T}\nabla_{H}T+\beta_{S}\nabla_{H}S],\partial_{z}v)_{L^{2}}dt-4\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}(\partial_{z}\hat{F}_{\sigma}^{v}(U),\partial_{z}v)_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle+4\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big(\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\Big[\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}(a_{3}^{K}\nabla_{3}(w(v)+w_{s}))+K*[(u_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3})(w(v)+w_{s})]\Big],\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}dt

\displaystyle+2\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)-\partial_{z}\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},{L^{2}})}^{2}dt+4\|[\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)-\partial_{z}\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}]^{*}\partial_{z}v\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},{L^{2}})}^{2}dt

\displaystyle+4\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\int_{\mathcal{S}}\Big[\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)(\phi_{k})-\partial_{z}{\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}}\Big]\boldsymbol{\cdot}\partial_{z}v\>d\beta_{t}^{k}.

(6.48)

Thus, reasoning as in subsection
6.2
,

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|\partial_{z}v(\eta)\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2})(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4})ds\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|[\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)-\partial_{z}\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}]^{*}\partial_{z}v\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},{L^{2}})}^{2}ds\Big].

(6.49)

It remains to estimate the last term on the RHS. Using the notations of subsection
6.2
, notice that,

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|[\partial_{z}\hat{G}_{\sigma}^{v}(U)-\partial_{z}\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}]^{*}\partial_{z}v\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},{L^{2}})}^{2}ds\Big]\\
\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\Big|\Big(\partial_{z}\psi_{k}-\partial_{z}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v)-\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w(v)),\partial_{z}v\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|^{2}ds\Bigg]\\
\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[(1+\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4})\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})ds\Bigg],

(6.50)

so that

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|\partial_{z}v(\eta)\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\mathcal{S}}|\nabla\tilde{v}|^{2}|\tilde{v}|^{2}+\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2})(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4})ds\Big].

(6.51)

Hence, the stochastic Grönwall lemma yields, with
\eta<\zeta<l_{k}\wedge T
again,

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[0,l_{k}\wedge T]}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{0}^{l_{k}\wedge T}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\nabla_{3}\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\>ds\Big]\leq C_{k}\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|\partial_{z}v(0)\|_{L^{2}}^{4}\Big].

(6.52)

\square

6.5
Globality-in-time of the solution

Our final argument for showing the existence of a global pathwise solution is inspired by what was proposed in
[
1
]
(see the arguments p.38-39), yet different. In the following, we use a result that is similar to Proposition 4.1 of
[
1
]
[Part 4], which we prove in the appendix (Appendix
A
): let
T>0
, and define two stopping times
\eta,\zeta<\tau\wedge T
, such that
\eta<\zeta
. Then the following holds,

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{s\in[\eta,\zeta]}\|v\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|v(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|w(v)\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\Big].

(6.53)

Moreover, remark that by Hölder inequality and Sobolev embeddings,

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big]\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|v\|_{V}^{2})\Big]+\xi\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\Big].

(6.54)

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\|w(v)\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq\int_{-h}^{0}\|w(v)\|_{L^{4}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{4}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}^{2}dz

\displaystyle\leq\Bigg(\sup_{[-h,0]}\|w(v)\|_{L^{4}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}^{2}\Bigg)\Bigg(\int_{-h}^{0}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{4}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}^{2}dz\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg(\int_{-h}^{0}\|\nabla\boldsymbol{\cdot}v\|_{L^{4}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}^{2}dz\Bigg)\Bigg(\int_{-h}^{0}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{4}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}^{2}dz\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C\Bigg(\int_{-h}^{0}\|v\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}\|v\|_{H^{2}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}dz\Bigg)\Bigg(\int_{-h}^{0}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{H^{1}(\mathcal{S}_{H})}dz\Bigg)

\displaystyle\leq C\|v\|_{H^{1}}\|v\|_{H^{2}}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{H^{1}}\leq C_{\xi}\|v\|_{V}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}+\xi\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}.

(6.55)

Thus,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|w(v)\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S})}^{2}\Big]\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\|_{V}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{H^{1}}^{2}\Big]+\xi\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\Big].

(6.56)

Using those estimates in equation (
6.53
), we reach

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|v\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds\Big]\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|v(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|v\|_{V}^{2}\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\|\partial_{z}v\|_{H^{1}}^{2})ds\Big].

(6.57)

Therefore, by the stochastic Grönwall lemma of
[
3
]
(rather than the one of
[
28
]
), the following holds a.s.,

\sup_{[0,l_{k}\wedge T]}\|v\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{0}^{l_{k}\wedge T}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds<\infty.

(6.58)

Since
\lim_{k\rightarrow\infty}\mathbbm{P}(l_{k}=\tau)=1
, we deduce that, almost surely,

\sup_{[0,\tau\wedge T]}\|v\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{0}^{\tau\wedge T}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds<\infty.

(6.59)

Thus,
\tau=\infty
a.s.
The aforementioned solution to the problem
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
is hence global-in-time, with values almost surely in the space

L_{loc}^{2}\Big([0,+\infty),\mathcal{D}(A)\Big)\cap C\Big([0,+\infty),V\Big).

\square

6.6
Continuity in probability with respect to the initial data and the noise operator

This subsection concerns the continuity of the solutions of
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
with respect to initial data and to the noise. We prove the second point of Theorem
2
below.

Let
U_{0}^{n}
,
U_{0}
,
\sigma^{n}
,
\sigma
,
U^{n}
,
U
and
R^{n}
as in the second point of Theorem
2
, and let
T>0
. Remark that,

\displaystyle R^{n}\in L^{2}\big([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A)\big)\cap C\big([0,T],V\big),\hskip 9.24994pt\forall T>0\text{ a.s.}

(6.60)

Let
\eta<\zeta
two positive stopping times. Reasoning as in the proof for uniqueness in subsection
5.2
, we infer that

\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|R^{n}\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R^{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds\Big]\leq\mathbbm{E}\Big[\|R^{n}(0)\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|R\|_{V}^{2}\Big(1+\|U\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+\|U^{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}\Big)ds\Big]\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\delta_{\sigma}^{n}(1+\|U\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+\|U^{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+(\beta_{\sigma}^{n})^{2})+(\delta_{\sigma}^{n})^{2}(\beta_{\sigma}^{n}+1)ds\Big],

(6.61)

where

\displaystyle\delta_{\sigma}^{n}:=\sup_{[0,T]}\|\sigma^{n}-\sigma\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H^{4})}^{2}+\|a_{3}-a_{3}^{n}\|_{H^{1}([0,T],H^{3})},

(6.62)

\displaystyle\beta_{\sigma}^{n}:=\sup_{[0,T]}(\|\sigma\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H^{4})}^{2}+\|\sigma^{n}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H^{4})}^{2})+\|a_{3}\|_{H^{1}([0,T],H^{3})}+\|a_{3}^{n}\|_{H^{1}([0,T],H^{3})}.

In addition, we define

\mathcal{N}(\eta,\zeta;X):=\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|X\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|X\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds,

(6.63)

and denote by

\tau_{k,n}^{*}:=\inf\{t>0\>|\>\mathcal{N}(0,t;U)+\mathcal{N}(0,t;U^{n})>k\}.

(6.64)

Using equation (
6.61
) with
(U^{1},U^{2},\tau^{\prime})=(U,U^{n},\tau_{k,n}^{*})
, by the stochastic Grönwall lemma of
[
3
]
, we infer that there exists a constant
C_{0}
such that, for all
\epsilon,K>0
, for all integer
k>0
,

\mathbbm{P}(\mathcal{N}(0,\tau_{k,n}^{*}\wedge T;R^{n})\geq\epsilon)\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\epsilon}e^{C_{0}K}(\|R_{0}^{n}\|_{V}^{2}+\delta_{\sigma}^{n})\\
+\mathbbm{P}\Big(\int_{0}^{\tau_{k,n}^{*}\wedge T}(\|U\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}+\|U^{n}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2})ds+\chi_{n}\geq K\Big),

(6.65)

where
\chi_{n}:=T\delta_{\sigma}^{n}(1+\beta_{\sigma}^{n})+T(1+(\beta_{\sigma}^{n})^{2})
. Since
\delta_{\sigma}^{n}
and
\beta_{\sigma}^{n}
are bounded, so is
\chi_{n}
. Then, denote by
\hat{\chi}=\sup_{n}\chi_{n}
, and set
K=k+1+\hat{\chi}
, so that

\mathbbm{P}(\mathcal{N}(0,\tau_{k,n}^{*}\wedge T;R^{n})\geq\epsilon)\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\epsilon}e^{C_{0}(k+1+\hat{\chi})}(\|R_{0}^{n}\|_{V}^{2}+\delta_{\sigma}^{n}).

(6.66)

Hence, by choosing
k_{n}=\Big\lfloor\frac{-\ln(\|R_{0}^{n}\|_{V}^{2}+\delta_{\sigma}^{n})}{2C_{0}}\Big\rfloor\rightarrow\infty
for a large enough
n
, we find that

\mathbbm{P}(\mathcal{N}(0,\tau_{k_{n},n}^{*}\wedge T;R^{n})\geq\epsilon)\leq\frac{C_{0}}{\epsilon}e^{C_{0}(1+\hat{\chi})}\sqrt{\|R_{0}^{n}\|_{V}^{2}+\delta_{n}}\rightarrow 0.

(6.67)

As the solutions
U
and
U^{n}
are global-in-time a.s.,
\tau_{k_{n},n}^{*}\rightarrow\infty
in probability. Therefore,

\mathbbm{P}(\mathcal{N}(0,T;R^{n})\geq\epsilon)\rightarrow 0,

(6.68)

that is to say
U^{n}\rightarrow U
in probability, in the space
C([0,T],V)\cap L^{2}([0,T],\mathcal{D}(A))
.

\square

Appendix A
Appendix 1: Proof of equation (
6.53
) – An estimate on the velocity and the temperature

We show equation (
6.53
), which is similar to Proposition 4.1 of
[
1
]
, and which we use in the final globality argument. The result is the following lemma,

Lemma A.1
.

Let
T>0
a real number. Let
U=(v,T,S)^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}
the solution of either
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
or
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
– equations (
3.16
) and (
3.22
) – given by the second point of Theorem
1
, and
\tau_{0}
its associated a stopping time. In addition, let two stopping times
\eta,\zeta<\tau_{0}\wedge T
, such that
\eta<\zeta
. Then,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Big[\sup_{s\in[\eta,\zeta]}\|v\|_{V}^{2}\Big]+\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds\Big]\leq

\displaystyle C\mathbbm{E}\Big[1+\|v(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds+\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|w(v)\partial_{z}v\|_{L^{2}}^{2}ds\Big].

(A.1)

Proof:
We will prove this lemma for the problem
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
, the proof for
(\hat{\mathcal{P}}_{K})
being similar. Remind first the definition of
(\mathcal{P}_{K})
,

\partial_{t}U^{*}+\Big[AU^{*}+B(U^{*})+\Gamma U^{*}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p+F_{\sigma}(U^{*})\Big]dt=G_{\sigma}(U^{*})dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma}.

(A.2)

Then, let
\theta=(T,S)^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}
, so that

\displaystyle\partial_{t}v^{*}+\Big[Av^{*}+B(v^{*})+\Gamma v^{*}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p+F_{\sigma}^{v}(v^{*})\Big]dt

\displaystyle=G_{\sigma}^{v}(v^{*})dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma},

(A.3)

\displaystyle\partial_{t}\theta+\Big[A\theta+B(v^{*},\theta)+F_{\sigma}^{\theta}(\theta)\Big]dt

\displaystyle=G_{\sigma}^{\theta}(\theta)dW_{t},

(A.4)

where
F_{\sigma}=:(F_{\sigma}^{v},F_{\sigma}^{\theta})^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}
and
G_{\sigma}=:(G_{\sigma}^{v},G_{\sigma}^{\theta})^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}
. Let
t>0
, and apply Itō’s lemma with
\|.\|_{V}^{2}
and
\|.\|_{H}^{2}
,

\displaystyle d_{t}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}=

\displaystyle-2(Av_{n}^{*},Av_{n}^{*}+B(v_{n}^{*})+\Gamma v_{n}^{*}+\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p+F_{\sigma}^{v}(v_{n}^{*}))_{H}dt

(A.5)

\displaystyle+2(Av_{n}^{*},G_{\sigma}^{v}(v_{n}^{*})dW_{t}-\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}dp_{t}^{\sigma})_{H}+\|G_{\sigma}^{v}(v_{n}^{*})-\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}dt,

\displaystyle d_{t}\|\theta_{n}\|_{H}^{2}=

\displaystyle-2(\theta_{n},A\theta_{n}+B(v_{n}^{*},\theta_{n})+F_{\sigma}^{\theta}(\theta_{n}))_{H}dt+2(\theta_{n},G_{\sigma}^{\theta}(\theta_{n})dW_{t})_{H}+\|G_{\sigma}^{\theta}(\theta_{n})\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},H)}^{2}dt.

(A.6)

Also, remind that the regularising properties of
K
lead to

\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{H}^{2}\leq C(1+\|U\|_{H}^{2}),\hskip 9.24994pt\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{V}^{2}\leq C(1+\|U\|_{V}^{2}).

(A.7)

Estimate of the bounded variation terms

For the advection term we have

\displaystyle(Av_{n}^{*},B^{n}(v_{n}^{*}))_{H}

\displaystyle\leq\|Av_{n}^{*}\|_{H}\|B^{n}(v_{n}^{*})\|_{H}\leq\xi\|AU_{n}^{*}\|_{H}^{2}+C_{\xi}\Big(\|v_{n}^{*}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w_{n}^{*}\partial_{z}v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}\Big).

(A.8)

Moreover, for all
\xi>0
,

\displaystyle|(Av_{n}^{*},\Gamma^{n}(v_{n}^{*}))_{H}|+|(Av_{n}^{*},(\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p)^{n})_{H}|

\displaystyle\leq C\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{V}\leq C_{\xi}(1+\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}.

(A.9)

For
F_{\sigma}^{v}
, by Young’s inequality,

\displaystyle(Av_{n}^{*},F_{\sigma}^{v}(v_{n}^{*}))_{H}\leq M_{\sigma}^{1}+M_{\sigma}^{2}+C\|Av_{n}^{*}\|_{H}\leq M_{\sigma}^{1}+M_{\sigma}^{2}+C_{\xi}+\xi\|Av_{n}^{*}\|_{H},

(A.10)

with

\displaystyle|M_{\sigma}^{1}|

\displaystyle\leq|b(v_{n}^{*},v_{s},Av_{n}^{*})|\leq C\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{1/2}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{3/2}\leq C_{\xi}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}+\xi\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

(A.11)

\displaystyle M_{\sigma}^{2}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\mu,\nu}\nabla_{3}\boldsymbol{\cdot}a_{3}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*},Av_{n}^{*})_{L^{2}}=-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\mu,\nu}\partial_{i}(a_{ij}\partial_{j}v_{n,k}^{*}),-\mu_{l}\partial_{ll}v_{n,k}^{*})_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\mu,\nu}\partial_{l}(a_{ij}\partial_{j}v_{n,k}^{*}),-\mu_{l}\partial_{il}v_{n,k}^{*})_{L^{2}}

\displaystyle=-\frac{1}{2}(D_{\mu,\nu}(a_{3,ij}\partial_{jl}v_{n,k}^{*}),-\mu_{l}\partial_{il}v_{n,k}^{*})_{L^{2}}+C_{\xi}\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}+\xi\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2},

(A.12)

where
D_{\mu,\nu}=Diag(\mu,\mu,\nu)
, and using Einstein’s notation convention of summation over repeated indices, with
i,j,l\in\{x,y,z\}
and
k\in\{x,y\}
. We also used the notation
\mu_{x}=\mu_{y}=\mu
and
\mu_{z}=\nu
.

Estimate of the martingale terms

Let
\psi_{k}=\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v+\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{s}]+A\phi_{k}^{H}+C\phi_{k}^{H}
and
\psi_{k}^{w}=\mathbf{P}K*[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}w_{s}]+A\phi_{k}^{z}
, so that

\frac{1}{2}\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(v_{n}^{*})-\mathcal{M}^{U,\sigma}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}=\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}-\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}]-\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{V}^{2}\\
\leq\frac{1}{2}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}\|_{V}^{2}+\|\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}]\|_{V}^{2}+2C\underbrace{|(\nabla_{3}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}),\nabla_{3}(\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}))_{L^{2}}|}_{\leq C|b(\phi_{k},v_{n}^{*},\Delta_{3}(\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}))|}\\
+\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{V}^{2}+C\underbrace{|(\nabla_{3}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k},\nabla_{3}(\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}))_{H}|}_{\leq C\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{H}\|\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}\|_{H^{2}}}+C|(\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}],\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k})_{V}|.

(A.13)

Additionally, remark that

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}(\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}],

\displaystyle\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k})_{V}\leq C_{\xi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{V}^{2}+\xi\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}.

Moreover, for all
\xi>0
,

\displaystyle\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}

\displaystyle\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}]\|_{V}^{2}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|D_{\mu}^{1/2}\nabla_{3}(\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|(\nabla_{3}\phi_{k})^{\scriptscriptstyle\rm T}.\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3\times 2})}^{2}+(1+\xi)\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|D_{\mu}^{1/2}\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}(\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*})\|_{L^{2}(\mathcal{S},\mathbbm{R}^{3\times 2})}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}+(1+\xi)(D_{\mu,\nu}a_{3,ij}\partial_{il}v_{n,k}^{*},\mu_{l}\partial_{il}v_{n,k}^{*})_{H},

where we used Einstein’s notation with
i,j,l\in\{x,y,z\}
and
k\in\{x,y\}
again. Hence, for all
\xi>0
,

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\|G_{\sigma}^{n}(v_{n}^{*})-\mathcal{M}^{U_{n},\sigma}\|_{\mathcal{L}_{2}(\mathcal{W},V)}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq-M_{\sigma}^{2}+C_{\xi}(1+\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})+\xi\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}.

(A.14)

Thus, using the equation (
A.5
), we reach

d_{t}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}+\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dt\leq C\Big(\|v_{n}^{*}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v_{n}^{*}\|_{H}^{2}+\|w_{n}^{*}\partial_{z}v_{n}^{*}\|_{H}^{2}\Big)\\
+2(v_{n}^{*},G_{\sigma}^{v}(v_{n}^{*})dW_{t})_{H}+C(1+\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})dt.

(A.15)

For all
T>0
and all stopping times
0\leq\eta<\zeta\leq T
, by integrating, then taking the supremum and the expectation, we have

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}]+\mathbbm{E}[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dr]

\displaystyle\leq\mathbbm{E}[\|v_{n}^{*}(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}]+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v_{n}^{*}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w_{n}^{*}\partial_{z}v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dr\Big]

(A.16)

\displaystyle+C\mathbbm{E}[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}(1+\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})dr]+\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta)}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{s}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{7}^{k}d\beta_{s}^{k}\Big|\Bigg].

Furthermore,

\displaystyle\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\Big|\int_{\eta}^{\boldsymbol{\cdot}}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}I_{7}^{k}d\beta_{r}^{k}\Big|\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\underbrace{\Big|\Big(\psi_{k}+\psi_{k}^{w}-\mathbf{P}[\phi_{k}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{3}v_{n}^{*}]-\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k},Av_{n}^{*}\Big)_{H}\Big|^{2}}_{\leq C(\|\psi_{k}\|_{H}^{2}+\|\phi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2}(1+\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})+\|\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}\pi_{k}\|_{H}^{2})\|Av_{n}^{*}\|_{H}^{2}}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\Bigg(\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}(1+\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg(\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|Av_{n}^{*}\|_{H}^{2}ds\Bigg)^{1/2}\Bigg]

\displaystyle\leq C_{\xi}\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}(1+\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2})\Bigg]+\xi\mathbbm{E}\Bigg[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|U_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}ds\Bigg].

(A.17)

Hence, using Lemma
4.1
,

\mathbbm{E}[\sup_{[\eta,\zeta]}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{V}^{2}]+\mathbbm{E}[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v_{n}^{*}\|_{\mathcal{D}(A)}^{2}dr]\leq C\Big(1+\mathbbm{E}[\|v_{n}^{*}(\eta)\|_{V}^{2}]\Big)\\
+C\mathbbm{E}\Big[\int_{\eta}^{\zeta}\|v_{n}^{*}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}+\|w_{n}^{*}\partial_{z}v_{n}^{*}\|_{L^{2}}^{2}dr\Big].

(A.18)

That is the result we seek.

\square

Appendix B
Appendix 2: Energy estimates for Theorems
3
&
4

Now we give a sketch of the proof for the energy estimates for the problems
(\mathcal{P}^{\gamma_{r}}_{EB})
and
(\mathcal{P}^{\gamma_{r}}_{EV})
– equations (
3.32
) and (
3.31
). These are used in the proof of Theorem 2 and 3, which run similarly as for Theorem 1. Therefore, the other steps, including the straightforward existence of Galerkin solutions, are omitted.

B.1
Energy estimate for the problem
(\mathcal{P}^{\gamma_{r}}_{EB})
(Theorem
3
)

Assume that
\gamma_{r}>1
. For the energy estimates, we only need to check for the pressure term, the noise terms and the additional correction terms
\frac{1}{2}\mathbf{P}\nabla\boldsymbol{\cdot}\check{a}[\nabla w]
and
\mathbf{P}\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}
.

Covariation correction term

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}(\mathbf{P}\nabla\boldsymbol{\cdot}\check{a}[\nabla w(v_{n}^{*})],v_{n}^{*})_{H}=-\frac{1}{2}(\check{a}[\nabla w(v_{n}^{*})],\nabla v_{n}^{*})_{L^{2}}

and
\|\mathbf{P}\mathcal{C}_{\sigma}\|_{H}\leq\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\phi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}\leq(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\phi_{k}\|_{L^{\infty}}^{2})^{1/2}(\sum_{k=0}^{\infty}\|\phi_{k}\|_{H^{4}}^{2})^{1/2}\leq C.

Bounded variation pressure term

\displaystyle\Big|\Big(

\displaystyle U_{n}^{*},\frac{1}{\rho_{0}}\mathbf{P}\nabla_{H}p\Big)_{H}\Big|\leq\Big|\Big(g\>\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}{(\beta_{T}T_{n}+\beta_{S}S_{n})dz^{\prime}},v_{n}^{*}\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big|\Big(\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}\Big[v_{s}\boldsymbol{\cdot}\nabla_{H}w(v_{n}^{*})+w_{s}\partial_{z}w(v_{n}^{*})\Big]dz^{\prime},v_{n}^{*}\Big)_{L^{2}}\Big|

\displaystyle+\Big(\nabla_{H}\int_{z}^{0}\Big[\frac{1}{2}\nabla\boldsymbol{\cdot}\hat{a}[\nabla v_{n}^{*}]+\frac{1}{2}\nabla\boldsymbol{\cdot}\hat{\hat{a}}[\nabla w(v_{n}^{*})]-\alpha(-\Delta)^{\gamma_{r}}w(v_{n}^{*})\Big]dz^{\prime},v_{n}^{*}\Big)_{L^{2}}
